Generated public types remain unchanged and pass Rust contract equality (`rollback-history-wire.log`). Review corrected a permissive draft continuation after rollback with a direct fence; no extra state machine or duplicate AGENTS rule was needed.

Pre-worker content initialization failure history now imports without inventing cancellation or execution authority. The source owner records exact `Failed`/`content_initialization_cancelled` evidence only when its reservation is cleaned up before worker hand-off; ordinary queued-waiter cancellation is not equivalent. The existing validator admits only that tuple and one exact initializing failure step, retaining common identity/plan checks and refusing metrics, changed targets, earlier steps and Guardian/recovery fields. An optional public failure point preserves the reason and is omitted for successes. No database schema, persistence owner, queue or UI change. The actual HTTP regression first fails ordinary import availability (`content-init-history-red.log`). Full two-thread consumers then pass **916 app / 129 API tests**, with six ignores in each, including three new owner groups, four importer checks and the extended authenticated source-free reopen journey (`content-init-history-consumers.log`). **88 desktop tests / 479 frontend tests** also pass, with one existing Guardian TODO; TypeScript, generated-contract equality, independent frozen-source review and scoped formatting pass (`content-init-history-{desktop,frontend,wire,format}.log`). Broader failed/recovery-bearing history remains open.

Successful Generic install/content history now imports through an immutable install-owned evidence store, closing the dispatch gap recorded below. Original raw schema, publication checkpoints, target projection, structured-token rules and full-width counters remain strict; source-global version records and selected-instance content bind through existing atomic publication/recovery/replay. Authenticated bounded reads reuse completed/live instance mappings and never create queue, readiness, launch or content authority. Full two-thread verification passes **909 app / 129 API tests**, with six ignores in each (`install-history-consumers.log`), including all nine owner checks, four import checks, publication/recovery failures and a real authenticated copy/reopen journey. **479 frontend tests pass**, with one existing Guardian TODO (`install-history-frontend-verified.log`); TypeScript and scoped Rust formatting pass. Independent final review is clear. Failed/cancelled and recovery-bearing history remain unimplemented; full cutover and parity are incomplete. See [history import](history-import.md).

The same slice passes **88 desktop tests** and generated-contract equality (`install-history-desktop.log`, `install-history-wire-check.log`). Recovery commit `7e0b03b3` is pushed directly to `main`. Current recovery correction adds inclusive 128-row/8-MiB Performance pending bounds, transactional admission/readback and bounded indexed active-command restoration. Existing oversized inventories are preserved and refused, never truncated. V1 migration bytes are unchanged; V2 adds only an index. Exact settlement may reduce an oversized inventory. Installation shutdown now joins its four queue-owned observer paths after TaskOwner shutdown and before file preservation. Registration/sealing is serialized, cancellation retains join handles, completed handles are reaped, and concurrent drains cannot lose a wakeup. Setup drops its queue clone before waking its response waiter. The current-thread real-root regression first passes without an observer, then reproduces `NoEffect(Busy)` with an unpolled scheduler; the correction passes immediate reopen, cancelled/concurrent drain and late-registration refusal. All 29 queue tests and scoped formatting pass; independent reviews are clear (`queue-observer-{red,green,suite}.log`, `recovery-bounds-format.log`). This demonstrates a lifetime gap, not proof of the exact original hosted event's cause.

Hosted [run36381431343](https://github.com/mateoltd/axial/actions/runs/36381431343) passes application and delivery for `aad07cd0`, including readiness reuse and exact recovery-proof validation (`readiness-recovery-hosted-ci.log`). Prior automatic-workflow corrections pass **853 app / 123 API tests**, with five ignores in each (new ignored API helpers are exercised by their parents), plus four focused API startup/process journeys and scoped formatting (`background-parity-{consumers,api-focused,format}.log`). Independent reviews cover production and the final fixtures. Same-profile benchmark startup now checkpoints overflow durably before scheduling and isolates a candidate's missing suite/invalid captured request without swallowing storage, corruption or admission failures. Desktop consumer verification also passes **88 tests** (`background-parity-desktop.log`). The implementation is committed as `d483b25b`; subsequent hosted verification is recorded above at `2f716df7`.

The real benchmark process fixture stops and settles the first run, verifies report/acknowledgement, both fixture PIDs gone and native cleanup, then exits only at the pending-run boundary. Ordinary startup resumes the remaining run without Resume/Tick commands, completes once, and another reopen preserves exactly two mappings/reports and one driver. The uncertain-session variant leaves the authenticated surviving-process mapping interrupted and the second entry pending across two normal quits; it creates no invented settlement or new session. Pre-fix disposable profiles may contain stale same-suite restart candidates; UUIDs and timestamps do not prove supersession, and this patch does not automatically choose authority among them.

Meaningful red checks reproduced overflow retry, an invalid driver blocking unrelated work, absent configured refresh and managed-Java preview refusal (`background-parity-red.log`, `import-java-red.log`). Filesystem simplification removes a second size observation after exact revision validation; 32 focused library-lifecycle tests pass (`revision-size-focused.log`). The default-concurrency Minecraft run finishes **953 passed / 9 failed**, all at existing 120-second deadlines, in 855.72 seconds (`revision-size-minecraft.log`). Readiness reuse and recovery-proof correction pass **837 app / 120 API / 88 desktop tests**, unchanged five/three app/API ignores, 35 focused coordinator tests and one grouped Setup journey. Independent reviews cover each source slice; formatting and API build pass (`readiness-recovery-{focused,consumers-final,desktop,format,build}.log`, `readiness-group-setup-final.log`). Frontend source and its previous 479-test checkpoint are unchanged. This is not full-parity completion.

Three serial measurements before and after use the same five-instance disposable profile, with zero benchmark drivers and no concurrent builds, UI activity or game launches. List times change from 45,201 / 45,290 / 45,055 ms to 35,274 / 34,674 / 34,801 ms: median 45,201 to 34,801 ms, an observed **23.0% reduction**. All six lists preserve exact ordered instance/version/readiness/action/error projections, including three launchable rows. Ordinary preflight medians remain 15,058 / 14,748 ms and detail 15,135 / 14,624 ms; neither path reuses a prior request's proof. These are local unoptimized-debug samples, not a general performance guarantee or solved latency. Both APIs exit0 normally; final SQLite quick_check is healthy. Logs: `readiness-group-{before,after,comparison}.log` and matching runtime logs. Old unresolved profiles remain untouched.

Instance-list enrichment groups requests while preserving output order and retains only one exact generation/library/version installation proof. Every reuse rechecks the publication lane and file revisions; account, settings, rules, Java probe and final instance fences remain per row. Ordinary preflight and launch/install admission remain fresh. Review caught a new queued-install liveness gap: the projected proof outlived the task-completion wake. Its existing owner now releases the proof before resuming the existing install queue, including early refusal paths. Deterministic red and green exercise actual queued work without provider traffic (`readiness-release-red.log`, `readiness-recovery-focused.log`). No global cache, scheduler or wire change. The group retains its reader lease longer, so conflicting installation waits until release.

Historical settlement review found startup skipping any envelope with integer version1 before exact proof validation. The corruption regression reproduces this through actual restoration (`settlement-scan-red.log`). Migration v4 changes only the unresolved index; applied v3 stays byte-identical. Every unacknowledged accepted/interrupted row now consumes bounded scan work and its complete observation is validated before restoration is waived. Reports remain optional; no acknowledgement, process adoption or settlement is invented. Repeated v3 upgrade/reopen, mixed valid/invalid evidence, exact row/byte budgets and sticky restoration refusal pass. Ordinary acknowledged lifetime history stays excluded; exceptionally large reportless histories can now exhaust the existing 4096-row/32-MiB budget and fail preserved. The prior missing independent observed-settlement review is now completed, with this concrete defect corrected.

The first combined run passed 120 API tests but aborted in the music retry fixture. Native crash evidence identifies retained root cleanup during runtime cancellation, not stack overflow; the exact abort predicate is not established (`readiness-music-abort-stack.json`). The unchanged focused case passes. The fixture published its successful result before TaskOwner released its retained pin, yet could immediately dispose its temporary root. One existing idle wait now precedes disposal; all original assertions remain. The complete uncaptured rerun passes 837/120. No production guard, timeout or stack limit changed. Initial NoLane test failure was a missing fixture publication directory, corrected before behavioral checks; the first desktop command incorrectly selected a nonexistent library target, then the actual desktop target passed. Low-space housekeeping removed only the validated incremental cache and 11 unused regenerable test binaries under the build lease; source, current binaries, profiles and evidence remain. Later local commands disable incremental cache growth. AGENTS.md now explicitly covers complete recovery-proof validation and release-before-wakeup ordering.

## Integrated behavior and evidence

### Import and settings

Ordinary instance import reuses creation/publication and a durable source-to-destination mapping. Accepted work survives preview closure; restart publication requires exact source readmission. Duplication and import share the copy implementation. Managed duplication retains independent destination rollback and ready/published content-witness fences (`witness-app-current.log`, `duplicate-witness-leaf-current.log`).

Ordinary canonical Fabric/Quilt/Forge/NeoForge imports now preserve their exact loader target and effective Vanilla/Custom settings through that same publication owner. Four focused cases and the actual HTTP copy/restart test pass (`manual-modded-import-app.log`, `manual-modded-import-api.log`), with source canaries and no fabricated installed readiness. Managed/provenance obligations and unresolved history stay blocked; remaining legacy coordinate compatibility and native modded-import acceptance are still open.

Genuinely empty Vanilla game-version declarations now resolve against one bounded fresh catalog lookup before native preview admission. Exact IDs are preserved; contradictions, malformed/reserved IDs and unknown versions remain unavailable. Existing agreeing declarations need no network, and provider failure does not hide unrelated usable import slices. Original records, fingerprints and publication receipts are unchanged; no source-library picker, custom executable adoption or installed readiness is introduced. Six consumer tests and three catalog HTTP tests pass in the current app checkpoint. Native composition retains source/selection/cancellation fences around provider I/O; real native missing-coordinate import remains unverified.

Profile-scoped interface preferences have separately revisioned storage and authenticated GET/PUT adapters, without invalidating config/launch fences. Eight storage tests and four route tests pass, including ignored SQL writes, dropped HTTP waiters, separate limits and unrelated state preservation. The exact 1 MiB export fixture normalizes to 1,171,622 bytes; the debug diagnostic measures ten config reads at 623 ms and ten route writes at 1,259 ms (`interface-preferences-cost.log`). This is a measured shared-document cost, not native UI performance. Native hydration, coalesced writes, exact uncertain-outcome reconciliation, theme compatibility and sealed import/reload now have 13 passing consumer tests within the full frontend checkpoint; the bounded macOS ARM64 debug persistence journey now passes above. Installed-release and other-platform persistence remain open. The existing terminal-attempt owner requests a renderer flush before closing task admission, or a recoverable discard seal for reset. No new journal or shutdown coordinator was added.

Offline and Microsoft identities, active selection, settings, flags and one completed source-bound receipt commit atomically. Credentials are never copied; imported Microsoft identities require sign-in. Replay preserves later edits and current telemetry consent. Actual HTTP tests cover rollback, stale inputs, source-independent receipt reads and waiter loss (`metadata-api-current.log`, `identity-readiness-api-current.log`). The UI uses receipt GET after unknown outcomes, fences both settings and account-selection revisions, and applies music/theme through existing owners. The delayed queued-music-write regression prevents old preferences from overwriting imported settings (`metadata-ui-final-frontend.log`, current frontend suite).

Bounded terminal reports, suites and drivers publish in the existing instance transaction with durable destination binding, immutable retry and source-scoped historical IDs. Preview prepares source-wide history once; unsupported history still blocks ordinary import. Historical benchmark records preserve old plans and references without launch intents or driver requests; all mutation and startup-resumption paths reject them. The authenticated HTTP journey imports twice, opens existing reads, refuses mutation, restarts the server, preserves source bytes/fingerprint and creates no live session or launch intent (`suites-api-current.log`). The developer-only driver view labels retained pending evidence as historical. See [history import](history-import.md) for bounds and unresolved nonterminal/unsupported obligations.

Terminal Performance journals now use the existing command table with a private historical discriminator and optional typed evidence on the existing status response. Original source schema/time, sequence, requested/actual action, terminal proof and destination binding are validated once and retained immutably. Publication and exact completed replay share the instance transaction; history cannot resume work and survives live-command pruning. Real Managed copy/reopen, changed evidence, ignored SQL insert rollback and consumer projection tests pass in the 765/97 checkpoint; 56 focused import tests pass (`history-import-focused.log`). The two initial real-reader fixture failures were fixed by migrating the rules table in that local helper, not relaxing production admission. Empty journal envelopes remain strictly checked. Signed rules cache adoption, nonterminal/unknown effects and unsupported source records still block full cutover.

Signed-rules adoption includes authenticated POST/status routes, rules-aware native preview and the existing import-dialog action in the 787/105/79/427 checkpoint. Destination policy/signature authority alone admits cache bytes: absent adopts, exact acknowledges, different conflicts. Immutable completion survives later refreshes without restoring the old cache. Bounded terminal global Refresh history retains source sequence/outcome, never invented timestamps or live operations; canonical string receipts preserve full u64 values. Receipt-aware Managed preparation, publication, recovery and replay recheck the same fact. Source canaries, dropped waiters, offline status, storage rollback, trust refusals, large sequences and malformed contracts are covered. Native projection resolves outside its selection lock. Definite 409/422 refusals retain safe causes; uncertain outcomes reconcile through GET only. Real native signed-rules import interaction and full cutover remain unverified.

Saved skins now use the skin library's existing SQLite owner and a separate completed source-bound receipt. Schema-3 metadata and normalized PNGs are validated before SQL; initial collisions require exact retained records. Replay preserves later edits/deletions and historical applied timestamps without applying a skin to an account. The UI imports skins independently, reconciles uncertain outcomes through GET, and retries failed current-library refresh without another POST. Initial backend/HTTP evidence is in `skins-app-current.log` and `skins-api-current.log`; current suites and maximum-size evidence are above. Full-profile cutover remains unavailable.

The necessary predecessor bridge exports only its two browser-local preference keys through an Advanced Settings download. The replacement's existing profile-import dialog validates the file, requires explicit source association, and resolves account, saved-skin and instance references through completed receipts and current owners. Missing/deleted/unmapped references block application. Browser import retains synchronous write/readback/rollback; native import drains existing drafts, rechecks reference evidence and commits the replacement through the profile preference owner before reload. Unknown outcomes remain sealed and reconcile by reading, never replaying writes. Native file selection, instance-route mapping and reload now pass the synthetic macOS ARM64 debug journey above; account/skin reference variants and other platforms remain separate. No backend restart, new journal or cross-tab atomicity is claimed. The superseded console-only export helper was removed.

### Installation, content and instances

Content and pack operations use the existing install queue, content transactions and instance setup intents. Queue admission/progress, persisted setup retry, pristine queued cleanup and explicit pending-setup deletion pass the composed checkpoint (`content-queue-app-current.log`, `content-queue-api-current.log`). Deletion UI now follows the exact retained operation status and removes/navigates only after confirmed removal (`import-deletion-frontend-current.log`). Lost mutation responses do not authorize blind replay.

Creation reuses an exactly verified installed version without reinstalling it, exposes artifact repair and preserves installed indicators. Display lists use the install owner's lightweight projection; creation and launch retain full selected-target verification. Automatic availability describes the preferred loader build, not an unrelated installed build. Beta labels, neutral Unknown semantics and Quilt compatibility apply across automatic/explicit selection and exact creation (`identity-readiness-app-current.log`, `create-display-app-current.log`, `history-final-app-api.log`). Three loader response types are generated from Rust, with their runtime decoder retained.

Real loopback install/launch/stop/restart and benchmark-recovery journeys exercise authenticated routes, managed game/runtime publication and installed-version creation. Their game process is a fixture, not proof of real gameplay. The formerly intermittent Java provisioning failure was reproduced as a retained-ancestor staging reservation bug, corrected narrowly, and followed by five passing API repeats (`runtime-reservation-red.log`, `runtime-reservation-fs-current.log`, `runtime-fixed-api-current.log`, `runtime-fixed-api-repeat-1.log` through `runtime-fixed-api-repeat-5.log`). It is no longer an unexplained failure; those repeats attest their frozen checkpoint only.

### Files, reset and distribution

Portable staging retains bounded streaming and exact recovery ownership. Evidence includes 17 MiB content install/update/remove, 32 MiB music publication, and 513-member transactions across bounded staging windows (`app-api-review-current.log`, `content-transactions-current.log`). Named crash leftovers remain preserved for explicit handling. Native extraction/publication failures retain cleanup authority (`native-extraction-tests.log`, `native-publication-current.log`).

Reset preserves the normal profile marker and a durable interrupted-reset record. Current v2 records require corrected launch/lifecycle admission before publication; old v1 records cannot establish that prerequisite and remain preserve-only. Startup neither creates nor consults missing/partial metadata to make old evidence appear safe. Current-v2 interruption still requires fresh native confirmation before deleting the current isolated tree; the record alone is not deletion authority. Filesystem 203/API 95 cover both paths (`launch-recovery-full-fs.log`, `launch-recovery-app-api.log`). The earlier clear/new-marker crash gap and unused reopen protocol remain superseded. Real interrupted-reset dialog behavior is unverified.

Launch acceptance atomically saves its actual admitted instance/library/application-root physical binding before spawn. Startup restores exact target/shared-artifact exclusions before runtime/install recovery. This earlier checkpoint also refused normal shutdown while old process settlement was unknown; the later preserve-only Quit correction above supersedes that refusal without clearing historical fences. Switching and destructive lifecycle actions still refuse. Failed startup can settle its own filesystem effects and preserve/exit without claiming that an older process stopped. Exact terminal reports acknowledge completion in their existing transaction; a private intent column and indexes keep lifetime completed history out of unresolved-work capacity without removing duplicate keys. Legacy reconciliation is bounded, retains verified progress, and requests another startup when incomplete. Ignored/failed INSERT or UPDATE rolls back proof and acknowledgement together. Canonical/indexed proof, binding mismatch, fresh-process reconstruction and consumer preservation cases pass. No new recovery table, PID signalling or age-based clearing was introduced; neither surviving-process adoption nor automatic settlement is inferred from these checks.

The standalone API embeds only a build-verified generation. Schema v2 compacts the generated receipt without removing graph, file, hash or budget evidence; strict pretty-encoded v1 verification remains compatible with older generations/backups. Human-authored asset and budget authorities stay unchanged. Earlier shipped duplication was removed without changing global CSS bytes or raising budgets (`metadata-ui-deduplicated-build.log`, [prior-review Git history](architecture-review.md#prior-reviews)).

The skin renderer now exposes only its 18 used Three exports at the existing lazy boundary. Its chunk decreased from 728,258 to 515,199 bytes, with unchanged consumer/rendering code, shared-load/retry behavior and budget categories. Current typing, behavior tests, production verification and the browser check below pass.

All 122 baseline method/template pairs were structurally registered at the metadata checkpoint (`metadata-ui-route-inventory.log`, [wire parity](wire-parity-review.md)); registration is not behavior parity. Separate bounded checks recorded 29 source assets, 73 delivery-validator tests and frontend semantic lint (`assets-check.log`, `delivery-tests.log`, `frontend-semantic-current.log`). These do not establish an installed release.

## Real-interface evidence and limits

The next three paragraphs record historical loader failures, not current acceptance status. Their Forge gaps are superseded by the later qualified install/launch/Stop/restart evidence in `.rewrite-logs/forge-processors-runtime-acceptance.md`. NeoForge has equivalent evidence in `.rewrite-logs/neoforge-runtime-acceptance.md`. Neither proves native gameplay, all eras or later-source acceptance. Quilt integrity refusal remains open.

On 2026-10-02, six bounded official Quilt artifact checks reproduce a provider defect: Meta SHA-1 equals the digest of the raw Maven checksum sidecar rather than the JAR. The [pinned official generator](https://github.com/QuiltMC/update-quilt-meta/blob/cf90889394779ac7689a7b36f055695dc7893c56/src/main/java/org/quiltmc/meta/update/util/maven/MavenRepository.java) hashes the downloaded checksum stream, matching that observed transformation. Checked loader0.30.1 Meta SHA-1 is `d057a81ff6252a795ba688ddd2431e5f88df1b35`; the JAR and sidecar contents give `2fa21d6438f63c8dd40f2e81290b0a09de1442b3`. Current loader0.31.0-beta.4 and two alternative tuples also mismatch. No trust-valid tuple or checksum bypass is claimed. The separate inherited parser gap in the [official non-obfuscated era](https://quiltmc.org/en/blog/2026-02-03-non-obfuscated-updates/) is corrected and behaviorally verified above; that does not repair upstream artifact integrity.

Fresh 787/105-checkpoint runtime findings: Quilt 0.30.1/MC 1.20.1 reached loader libraries 9/11, then strict integrity refusal; official Quilt Meta digests disagree with both actual official Maven bytes and their checksum sidecars. The mismatch is independently reproduced without profile writes; no checksum bypass is authorized. Forge 47.4.10/MC 1.20.1 passed base acknowledgement then rejected the official install profile's null optional path. A narrow ForgeModern correction now passes **42 installer tests**, formatting and independent review (`forge-null-path-tests.log`, `forge-null-path-format.log`), but is not in a live binary yet. Five outputless official client processors remain a separate required implementation gap. See [Forge findings](forge-loader.md) and `rules-retry-runtime-acceptance.md`. API PID 59230 exited zero on normal SIGINT after both failures; old APIs 30971/96772 remain untouched. Neither new loader was launched.

The next real loader wave is **failing**, not accepted. On API PID 21244, Quilt 0.30.1/MC 1.20.1 instance `b1c259ab-48eb-47bb-8211-33741365856d` reached base completion then failed at loader libraries 9/11 (queue `65872c7e-b9f0-4598-9f48-4fc939e324fa`, operation `bf31d885-4e95-4ecf-ac33-09c8d4df7d4d`). Forge 47.4.10/MC 1.20.1 instance `9b86aa5c-f466-4cf7-b0c6-0599caccc963` reached base completion and artifacts 1/1 then failed (queue `c28a5fb1-01db-4778-9c3f-78f766b3c0ba`, operation `28b89a8f-85df-4d23-8f74-da9c6c65c74f`). Both durable outcomes are failed; terminal alone was initially mistaken for success and explicitly corrected. Current public errors lose the cause. Preserve evidence and add narrow typed diagnostics before retrying (`forge-install-failed.png`); no launch was attempted.

On rebuilt API PID 30971, Quilt retry queue `fe8b218a-d8ac-4dff-836c-bf85c4917377`, operation `7963157f-0ea0-4309-a9a9-3449d3143dbc`, is retained as `settlement_required`, outcome null, before app checkpoint persistence and before loader-profile progress. No diagnostic warning identifies the cause. The native base witness exists but does not establish application acknowledgement. No live shutdown or record/file intervention was attempted. Forge retry `34144b7a-2ffb-4f0d-8285-c9a4735ce43c`, operation `07e3aeee-4fd6-4e72-b7b1-eb5c900ba815`, was removed through the UI before starting; durable outcome is `removed` with empty progress, and its instance remains. See `quilt-retry-settlement.png` and `quilt-forge-acceptance.md`. Neither loader has launch acceptance.

An actual gated preflight regression reproduced foreground `InstanceBusy` while only the owned Java probe was waiting (`preflight-read-admission-red.log`, 0 passed/1 expected failure). It releases/joins the probe and shuts down cleanly before asserting. The read-admission correction and final fences pass the 768/97 checkpoint; this proves/fixes source contention, not the exact causal path of the earlier UI409.

Latest resource/content run uses the same isolated profile. World backup and rename succeeded while idle; the backup and renamed source have identical SHA-256 `5cd89cb3e54c53355561eebd245d9ee19180a4b2c28350c4f3d886c4636eb998`. With real Vanilla Playing, backup and rename both visibly refused and created no busy-attempt destination. Stop returned Ready, persisted session `0b60b121-18c8-4f6d-8e8c-4ab23d858a54` as stopped (3,113 ms boot) with terminal acknowledgement, and API PID 78923 later exited zero. Screenshots: `recovery-world-backup.png`, `recovery-world-renamed.png`, `recovery-world-busy.png`, `recovery-world-stopped.png`. The world is a synthetic listing fixture, not playable-world evidence.

Real Fabric 0.19.5 for Minecraft 1.20.1 installed successfully into instance `6bf331ba-fb13-44e0-8e46-93ffe80cd285`. Sodium initially failed before any install POST because Rust omitted empty plan dependencies. After the decoder correction and rebuilt generation `e608f7cb7cd1`, the same UI flow succeeded: queue `98b455f2-dbd4-453e-8b6c-33bb392f61cd`, operation `9ac7af9c-b635-4471-9a63-5940727a233e`, terminal success. Mods shows Sodium enabled (`architecture-sodium-installed.png`); the 971,552-byte JAR SHA-1 is `bcdbf37d9494e405cba210d7a80ed58e756297b5`, matching the actual plan. The first Play rejected before spawn and created an empty Managed-absent rollback snapshot. After removing implicit bundle mutation, session `4a213ae5-7b95-40cd-b4a1-776f6214518b` reached Playing; the actual game log confirms Fabric and Sodium loaded (`performance-fabric-playing.png`). This is startup evidence, not game-window gameplay acceptance.

That run exposed two failures: exclusive content-read admission returned 503 while launch retained its lease; generic secret-like-string redaction altered the canonical Fabric report ID, so exact terminal acknowledgement rejected it. Stop exited the game, settled its tree and drained output, with an empty native cache, but left no terminal report (`performance-fabric-cleanup-refusal.png`). The old API/failed fixture remain preserved. Do not describe that run as proven native-file obstruction or successful clean stop. No records, user files or safety fences were bypassed.

Both corrections now pass a separate fresh-profile run: instance `d6926227-f5e6-47a3-a736-f6c1cef14435`, Fabric 0.19.5/MC 1.20.1, Sodium 0.5.13. Actual game logs confirm both loaders/mods; the Sodium SHA-1 remains `bcdbf37d9494e405cba210d7a80ed58e756297b5`. Refresh during Playing returns 200 for content and updates, without UI error (`typed-report-content-playing.png`). Session `9fbed338-269b-4664-a1da-06f8d52cbc97` stops to Ready, persists matching canonical report/scenario IDs and `terminal_ack=1`; report boot duration is 7,293 ms (`typed-report-content-stopped.png`). Both queue entries are terminal and content/Performance pending tables are empty. API PID 3233 then exits zero on normal SIGINT; PID 7345 reopens the same profile, keeps the report/ack and shows Ready plus enabled Sodium (`typed-report-content-restarted.png`). These are launcher/browser and actual process checks, not native game-window gameplay or crash-recovery acceptance.

The new 765/97 checkpoint passes a further narrow restart check. API PID 7345 exits zero on SIGINT; rebuilt PID 21244 serves port 62454 with the same isolated profile, Ready instance, enabled Sodium and ParityCheck (`history-readiness-restarted.png`). Read-only SQL retains the accepted/acknowledged session and no pending content/Performance rows. No game was launched in this check. The shell's inherited `RUST_LOG=warn` hid the informational listening line; idle thread sampling and the empty runtime log did not establish a startup stall. ## Active release blockers and next work

[Performance effect-intent recovery](performance-ui.md#effect-intent-remove-preserves-the-bundle) now has a genuine nonempty-profile HTTP/process journey: one Remove crashes after its durable marker but before removal, then ordinary recovery fails that same command, clears pending work and preserves the complete bundle/history through another reopen. No source change or mid-file-crash acceptance follows.

- Current native settings persistence and Quit/reopen have bounded isolated macOS ARM64 debug and [locally installed release-configuration evidence](native-auth.md#current-source-installed-vanilla-journey); ordinary Reset has earlier evidence. Interrupted Reset's explicit Preserve-files choice passes the bounded current fixture in [native reset](native-reset.md); other interruption boundaries, installed updates and other platforms remain open. The OAuth window selects a fresh nonpersistent macOS store because WKWebView ignores its configured directory; real sign-in/cookie isolation remain unverified. Intentional tradeoff: new macOS sign-in windows do not reuse browser cookies; durable account/refresh credentials are unchanged. Windows/Linux keep their profile directories.
- Complete native gameplay acceptance and resolve slow preparation after the passing browser-driven real Vanilla install/launch/logs/stop/restart journey.
- Extend resource acceptance to failure, cancellation/interruption and installed-platform folder actions. Four current macOS ARM64 debug folder destinations now have actual Finder evidence in [Library](library-ui.md). Ordinary managed/local Mods toggles, removal, update and restart have bounded interface evidence in [Mod files](mod-files.md); ordinary world backup, rename, deletion, in-use refusal and restart are recorded in [World resources](world-files.md). Populated Screenshots refresh/read/rename also passes. These do not prove playable-world loading or interrupted native file publication; earlier incomplete fixture-canary runs remain qualified in their original records.
- Complete current-app Performance and benchmark acceptance, including same-driver Resume, automatic restart, applicable native/platform reopen and exact session/report settlement. Keep unresolved file/process obligations fenced; persisted history alone must not authorize new execution. Predecessor cutover and imported-history continuation are superseded scope, not release gates.
- Complete outstanding feature-family failure/restart acceptance. Actual Unix fixture-process journeys already prove surviving game/descendant preservation, exact restored fences, refused replay/mutation and normal preserve-only shutdown across two reopens. Remaining native active-file/process interruption status/refusal and preserve-only Quit, real gameplay, Windows containment/crash behavior and other crash boundaries still need evidence; the bounded interrupted-startup Reset Preserve case passes separately. Legacy startup creates empty session/process maps and does not adopt surviving processes (`legacy/apps/api/src/bootstrap.rs`, `state/sessions/mod.rs`, `app.rs`); a new adoption system is not an established parity requirement. Existing tests neither adopt/settle unknown processes nor reconstruct lost native cleanup authority. Current-app accepted rows without physical binding or exact terminal proof remain unavailable. Never guess the current tree or clear by age, PID or a fresh key.
- Obtain installed native evidence on all four baseline artifact architectures, plus trusted release updater inputs and demonstrated signed installed update/restart behavior. Horizon restarts into an active unlocked Fedora44/KDE Wayland session; the existing RustDesk viewer fails to reconnect, so there is still no supported observable Linux GUI. The [verified media package](linux-package.md#released-packager-and-media-correction) now has joined build, payload, bounded no-override startup and actual plugin-decoding evidence. Preserve original runtime/protocol failures and remaining graphics warnings. Canonical isolated-profile GUI/HTTP/media/SSE, audible playback, visible icon presentation and ordinary lifecycle acceptance remain open; these controls are not installed acceptance.

## Preserved baseline and verification discipline

- Work continues on `main`; `feat/clean-rewrite` retains the integration checkpoint. Baseline commit is `2bda9b42c29d02828dd5a2b91d9cf8c8a431ffb0`.
- `legacy/` retains the original project, local generated output, dependencies and build output. The initial Git blob comparison checked all 817 relocated tracked files: none missing (`preferences-legacy-preservation.log`). Its then-present preference-export control/bridge is historical; compatibility-only bridge changes are subsequently removed at `93a000cb`. The reference tree is not a production dependency.
- No user installation, existing application profile, external library, credentials or release publication has been changed. Tests and UI checks use isolated profiles.
- Shared Cargo verification is serialized. Later changes never inherit earlier passing evidence. Architecture review is required at feature integration; the configured 30-minute schedule is active. [Architecture](architecture-review.md) and [adversarial](adversarial-review.md) reviews record decisions, not release acceptance. Package-specific notes supplement but do not override the blockers above.
